#2927. Preview: Surviving a pack of Wolves

We won't deny it: the last week or so has consisted of games that have been a 'must-win' for Utah. And tonight is included.

The team's play against Dallas was more on the promising side: fans saw Darryn Peterson start to find his groove and play like the #2 pick he is, Lauri Markkanen hit a mind-boggling eight triples, but it feels like Utah needs to play a perfect and synchronised game of basketball just to win a game of basketball.

The Jazz road trip concludes with a visit to see our divisional foe, along with former friends in Minnesota. The roster overhaul over the 2026 offseason added star LaMelo Ball to the mix, designed to alleviate the facilitating role for Anthony Edwards. They've even got a brand-spankin' new rebrand as the league adapts to the dying trend of minimalism. The Wolves are so unrecognisable that they even have Rudy Gobert looking like Montel Williams.

The addition of LaMelo and Kuminga has given them an early season boost. They hold a +5.1 rebound-per-game improvement compared to the baseline average of 49.2 rebounds per game last season. They've also maintained their defensive core to post a 106.6 defensive rating, second-best in the Association. After a hot 3-0 start, the Wolves have sizzled to a comfortable 7-3 record to sit at the 6th spot in the Western Conference.

Minnesota's rebounding is arguably Utah's biggest concern if they're chasing a win. They rank 2nd in the NBA in offensive rebound percentage (35.6%), making them a formidable foe for a team already missing Jusuf Nurkic. The team needs to stay disciplined, ensuring that every player sticks to boxing out.

It's especially important that the Jazz shooting woes need to be put on hold, or else they'll get punished on the other end. They have converted just 28% of their three-point attempts over the last three games (34-of-121). While they demonstrated minor improvement against Dallas, the overall efficiency continues to be a lingering issue. Missed shots could quickly translate into transition opportunities for the Wolves, while their offensive rebounding allows them to compensate for their own missed attempts.

And really, I don't know if it's possible to hold your own if Mo Bamba is another healthy scratch from the rotation, with a DNP-CD against Dallas, in favour of Tony Bradley. Perhaps his ankle flared up before tonight, but either way, Utah needs some size advantage.

NEW ORLEANS, LA – NOVEMBER 4: Jaxson Hayes #11 of the Utah Jazz goes up for the rebound during the game against the New Orleans Pelicans on November 4, 2026 at the Smoothie King Center in New Orleans, Louisiana. It feels like it's been years since the Jazz were a bottom-10 team in turnovers. The lack of winning experience, combined with inconsistent offensive flow, has left the Jazz with a 17.2% turnover rate, ranking them 26th in the league.

This was evident when the Jazz coughed up 21 turnovers in their NBA Cup loss to the Mavericks. Will Hardy specifically highlighted the team's concerns about handling the ball and maintaining an offensive flow:

The ball movement that was promised still hasn't come to fruition. Things seem stagnant, relying heavily on Keyonte and Lauri to create their own shots rather than having them come to them naturally. Yes, a large part of it is them missing their distributing big man, Nurkic (whom will likely miss the rest of November), but it seems like they're trying to force-feed their top guys more than anything else.

Minnesota already rank eighth in opponent turnover percentage (16.04%), with McDaniels and Kuminga pressuring opposing teams on the perimeter. The Jazz can't lose focus. They will need to become more decisive, make quicker reads, and avoid unnecessary dribbling or else they risk the Timberwolves running away with this one.

The serious advantage in Utah's deck of cards is Minnesota's absence of Anthony Edwards for the next couple of months. When the Wolves win, it comes from contributions from several players rather than relying on a dominant scorer. Utah should look to disrupt the Timberwolves' flow by forcing their second and third options into difficult situations and preventing them from getting comfortable in the early going.

Utah's job tonight will be limiting the role of All-Star LaMelo Ball along with Minnesota's array of perimeter shooters, including Terrence Shannon Jr. and Bones Hyland. Dosunmu exploded for 26 points in New Orleans a couple days ago, so he'll be expected to carry that momentum into tonight.

It's also important to highlight the progression of Jaden McDaniels, posting a career-high 17.1 points per game and a 51.1% field-goal percentage if the season ended today. McDaniels also ranks first with the best offensive rating in the fourth quarter (141.3).

There's little room for error if the Jazz hope to beat a proven Playoff team. It feels like this has been the story for weeks, but seriously, it's do-or-die time for the Jazz to save this season.

#2921. Lions vs. Dolphins final score, immediate winners, losers, reactions

The Miami Dolphins' Week 9 schedule brought the NFL's top-ranked offense to Hard Rock Stadium as the Detroit Lions came to South Florida. It was a tough matchup for a 1-6 team still trying to grow and develop with a roster full of rookies, but the Dolphins stood up and fought for all 60 minutes.

Detroit put the game away late, but with the over/under set at 50.5 points for the day, a combined 28-point total reflects strong defensive play from both teams.

The Dolphins defense showed up to play in this game. Hafley, the former Green Bay Packers defensive coordinator, clearly brought that experience to Miami and used it against the Lions today. The defense was ready to face the number one offense in the league and they did well for much of the game. They allowed one touchdown drive in the second half with a couple of big plays, but for the most part, Hafley's defense was ready and executed well.

Early in the season, frustration over Robinson's lack of production was growing. Over the past few weeks, he put that behind him, and on Sunday, he was a force throughout the game.

Continuing with the defensive theme, Uche was disruptive throughout the game as well. Uche set the tone early, disrupting the run, setting the edge, deflecting the pass that led to a Chris Johnson interception. and getting a sack. He was a factor in the game, even if things fell off a little in the second half.

Douglas dropped a late fourth-down attempt that would have given Miami a first down as they tried to come back, but aside from that, the rookie receiver had a day. He finished the game with six receptions for 70 yards.

Note: "Losers" is not a judgment on the talent or character of any individual. It is simply a categorization of the play in one game – no disrespect is intended or should be inferred

The Dolphins' punter has struggled this year, especially in terms of consistency. Also serving as the kickoff specialist, Pinion was flagged on the opening kickoff for failing to get the ball into the landing zone. In the second quarter, he shanked a punt for just 25 yards. It was not a banner day for the Dolphins' punter. Pinion did have a 46-yard pass to running back Kaytron Allen on a fake punt, which nearly pushed him off this list, but the season-long inconsistency keeps him here.

So many flags were thrown against the Dolphins this week, especially against the offensive line. Counting declined penalties, Miami was flagged 12 times in the game. It feels more like a rookies trying too hard more than an issue of players not paying attention.

Rookie receiver Chris Bell was just non-existent on Sunday. He was targeted one time and recorded no receptions. While Douglas continues to break out, Bell is struggling to produce. The potential is still there, and he will break out at some point, but right now, he is a complete afterthought for the offense.

Lions head coach Dan Campbell's first coaching job was with the Dolphins, starting as an intern, then as the tight ends coach, and ending as the team's interim head coach after Joe Philbin was fired. Campbell showed flashes of what he would become as a head coach, but he was not yet ready to get the job full time. There was definitely temptation there.

Campbell instead went to the New Orleans Saints as their tight ends and assistant head coach. Campbell moved to the Lions in 2021 and has worked therm through a rebuild into a team that should be a contender for a deep run in the NFC playoffs. After Campbell's interim stint, Miami hired Adam Gase, then Brian Flores, then Mike McDaniel, and now Jeff Hafley.

The Lions coaching staff also includes defensive coordinator Kelvin Sheppard, defensive line coach Kacy Rodgers, and outside linebackers coach David Carrao – all of whom were Dolphins players or coaches.

Willis is clearly bothered by the groin injury that has been on the injury report for the last couple of weeks. He just doesn't have the same mobility we have seen from him before. He tried a couple of times to run away from pressure, but he just didn't have the speed to get going. He showed solid understanding of the situation and threw the ball away at times, but the lack of scrambling ability was very noticeable in the game.

Pinion's struggles (and the fake punt) were mentioned above, but overall, the Dolphins' special teams still aren't special. They gave up a fake punt, Zane Gonzalez blasted the upright on a field goal attempt to end the half, and overall, it feels like Miami cannot trust their special teams this year.

#2917. San Antonio vs. Portland, Final Score: Spurs dispatch Trail Blazers, improve to 2-0 on road trip, 125-103

Both sides were on the second night of a back-to-back set, and the San Antonio Spurs handled the Portland Trail Blazers on the road. This was the first matchup between last year's first-round opponent and it was uncommonly physical for a November game.

The Spurs didn't shoot well early, but were the nastier team from tip-off, getting into the bonus in fewer than six minutes and forcing a few turnovers. Their quickness shifting into the lane tempted the Blazers into too many unsuccessful 3-point attempts which sparked transition, but they got sloppy, committing too many fouls and that bad habit persisted most of the first half.

The Trail Blazers stayed on their heels with relentless hustle, and then the Silver and Black cracked the code. Eventually, Victor Wembanyama also went on a tear that zapped whatever will the hosts had left. He had 29 points, seven rebounds and three assists. His biggest help came from Stephon Castle, who played a nice all-around game with 14 points, seven rebounds and five assists, plus Devin Vassell, who added 15 points and two steals.

Garbage time started when they built up a 32-point lead late in the third quarter.

The Trail Blazers were down one penetrator in Ja Morant, which affected their ability to bend the defense. On top of that, the schemes made Portland look ordinary at their downhill game and they never countered with feeding Donovan Clingan in the post. Their rebounding helped keep them in the game but that only lasted for so long.

The Spurs played with high intensity but in the first half, not much discipline. All those cheap fouls had three of them in foul trouble and prevented them from continuing their work in transition, giving the Blazers an opportunity to start creeping in through the door.

The Trail Blazers are a long, athletic team with good screen navigation, so getting by them into the lane can be a challenge. The Spurs' jump-shooting unfastened their defense, but they started playing more forcefully on the perimeter and made it tougher to maneuver. The Spurs were not phased and kept plugging away, shooting 77.3 percent in the restricted area.

It seems that Castle brings it when is playing his old teammate, Clingan. He was one of the team's best rebounders, and he was like a linebacker stopping the run game inside, especially from Deni Avdija.

It was a rough shooting night before the game broke open, and the team got a big lift from Julian Champagnie, making four 3-pointers, including two consecutive treys in the second quarter, when the team badly needed it.

Toumani Camara said the plan was to get physical with Wembanyama before the game, and they did, not giving him much room after catches, bumping him on the move and using some hand checks. This played a big part in him going eight-for-nine at the line. The extra stuff did not wear him down physically or mentally.

Exhibit A. of the game flipping like a light switch: the Spurs made 17 first-half field goals had 18 in the third quarter.

The Spurs eventually took an edge on the glass 13 and added 23 back-breaking second-chance points.

Carter Bryant got subbed into the game early in the second quarter, and his minutes were mostly negative because his aggressiveness can be used against him. He had three fouls in three minutes, yet coach Mitch Johnson gave him a chance to make up for it with extra time and later as well when the game was out of reach.

#2912. CeeDee Lamb’s fumble vs. the Colts is the most chaotic play of the NFL season

The 2026 NFL campaign is just reaching the midway point of the regular season.

But you might not find a wilder play than this fumble by Dallas Cowboys wide receiver CeeDee Lamb Sunday against the Indianapolis Colts.

With the visiting Cowboys facing a 3rd-and-12 in their own territory, while holding a 20-17 lead, Indianapolis brought the blitz against quarterback Dak Prescott. With several pass rushers in his face, Prescott got off a throw to Lamb along the right sideline. Because Prescott was unable to step into the throw, the pass was underthrown and Lamb had to break back to the football.

Which he did, making a diving catch in front of rookie cornerback Kenneth Harris.

And that is exactly when the chaos began.

Harris slipped on his own break, and that allowed Lamb enough separation to make the diving catch. But Harris never touchdown Lamb down, who alertly got to his feet and raced upfield, picking up a block as he sliced into the red zone.

That's when he was tackled, lost the ball, and following a challenge from Colts head coach Shane Steichen, Indianapolis took over possession.

The FOX Sports booth speculated that because he is a rookie, Harris believed in the moment that Lamb did not need to be touched down. After all, in the college game once a player goes to the ground with the football, they are down at that spot and the play is whistled dead.

Instead, he gave Lamb a chance to pick up additional yardage, only for the Dallas WR to fumble the ball away after what was, for a brief moment, a brilliant play.

And no one was happier for that fumble than Harris.

#2910. Meta chief strategy officer Dave Wehner leaves to join AI infrastructure startup Fireworks as CFO

Dave Wehner, Meta's chief strategy officer, is leaving the company for four-year-old AI startup Fireworks.

At Fireworks — an AI inference and model-training platform focused on open models that was valued at $17.5 billion during a Series D fundraising round in July — Wehner will assume the CFO role, he announced on X on Friday.

Wehner will be tasked with scaling the company's finances and operations, Fireworks CEO Lin Qiao said in a statement to CNBC.

"Fireworks has the right mission for the moment," Wehner wrote on X, adding: "I've become increasingly bullish on what they are building. There's a remarkable depth of technical talent here, coupled with a very practical focus on what customers need."

The AI startup, founded in 2022 by former Meta and Google employees, is a platform that lets developers customize and run open models for their businesses. Its latest investment round was led by Atreides Management, Index Ventures and TCV, but Fireworks is also backed by Nvidia and Sequoia Capital. Business Insider reported that it is in early talks to raise funds again at a possible $30 billion to $40 billion valuation.

Wehner joined Meta, then called Facebook, in 2012 and was the company's chief financial officer from 2014 to 2022.

"Dave helped guide Meta through tremendous growth and complexity over 14 years, and brought disciplined thinking to some of our hardest company-level decisions," Meta COO Javier Olivan said in a statement to CNBC.

Fireworks is notably smaller than Meta — the tech giant has about a $1.56 trillion market capitalization as of Friday's close — and its competitors, which include OpenAI and Anthropic.

"Dave told me he wanted to get back to a smaller company where he could build toward a big impact again," Fireworks' Lin wrote in a LinkedIn post on Friday. "He sees AI as the defining technology shift of his lifetime and wants to help shape what comes next."

The company's size has not made it immune to the risks and complications often associated with AI technology. In October, the company found "a security incident involving unauthorized use of internal Fireworks credentials," in which "an unauthorized third party accessed environment variables used in evaluation jobs in a limited number of customer accounts," it said in a press release.

"Based on our investigation at this time, we have not detected evidence of unauthorized access to customer models, datasets, training data, personal information, or inference traffic within the Fireworks security perimeter or control," the release said.

The Cleveland Cavaliers fell 109-97 to the New York Knicks on the second night of a back-to-back.

Grades are based on our usual expectations for each player.

He's been aggressive first, allowing his playmaking to come as a result of the defense overreacting to his gravity. That's led to some efficient games passing the ball, but we were all waiting for Mitchell to have a scorching hot game shooting it.

Mitchell went off for a season-high 37 points, and he was the catalyst behind Cleveland's momentary comeback in the fourth quarter. Mitchell was one of the only Cavaliers who had a pulse on offense tonight.

12 points, 9 rebounds, 5 assists, 3 turnovers

The Cavs waived Minix just days ago, before bringing him back only a few hours before tip-off. He responded with the best game of his young career, nailing his first three shots and ending with 8 points.

#2908. Brands are sponsoring IRL communities. Some experts say that could spell their end

On a brisk Saturday in October, more than 80 people try to shuffle into the small Corner Bookstore in New York City's Upper East Side. They're part of local literary community Lit Club NYC's quarterly book crawl. Today's event, where some attendees are "slightly over sci-fi" and others are in their "rom-com era," is sponsored by Tinder.

The Lit Club-Tinder partnership was facilitated by Offline, a platform for connecting hosts of gatherings across the U.S. with advertisers keen to sponsor their events. The goal is to help advertisers "deliver outsized media performance and drive growth," according to Offline's website. Offline founder Andrew Roth, 28, is also keen to help hosts ensure their gatherings are sustainable long term.

Kelly Chervin is just such a host. Chervin, 35, founded Lit Club in March 2025 as a way to meet other Latina readers and connect over literature. But she has bills to pay. "If we want to have a literary community be around sustainably," says Chervin, "then paid partnerships are part of that."

For the Tinder collab, Chervin makes $20 per attendee, and must throw in deliverables like social media posts highlighting the partnership. On Lit Club's Instagram, a recent post included the line "You can now discover events like these on the @tinder app!"

Whether community members will stay connected when a sponsor gets attached remains to be seen. Some experts are optimistic about the opportunity this kind of marriage presents, while others are certain it will eventually bring about communities' demise.

Either way, many intimate gatherings around the country are quickly adding a sales pitch.

Roth began his career by founding Gen Z-focused research and consulting company DCDX while studying at Vanderbilt University in 2020. The company has worked with major brands including Chipotle, Hinge and Spotify and was recently acquired by Visible Ventures for an undisclosed sum. Roth will stay on as a Board Advisor.

In 2024, while still at DCDX, Roth had the opportunity to work on an advertising campaign for fragrance company Le Monde Gourmand at in-person events with communities like those on which Offline now focuses.

He says it made him wonder: "Can we bring those [advertising] dollars into the real world and help these communities be preserved?" In September 2025, he and fellow entrepreneur Chris Banks officially launched Offline.

The company's platform enables hosts of gatherings across the country, from mom groups to run clubs to literary circles, to create a profile including details about how often events happen and how many people tend to show up. Brands can then use the site to match with profiles and events that fit their target audience and create a brief for a campaign that hosts can submit ideas to.

Offline now features more than 900 communities altogether, says Roth, with hosts making an average payout of $2,500 to $3,000 per event.

"The language we always use is 'intimacy at scale,'" he says.

For hosts like Chervin, the opportunity to generate revenue is critical. Lit Club's current income streams include ticketed events, a monetized newsletter and brand partnerships.

Chervin is choosy about the partnerships she takes on, she says. They have to feel organic. The Tinder tie-in, for instance, checks a couple of boxes: "Tinder really wants to move to in-person connection," she says as an example, which is at the core of her mission as well.

Michael Lombardo, 43, discovered bird watching in 2019 and was immediately struck by how fun and present the activity was. It made him feel "like the eight-year-old version of myself," he says, "where I was actually inhabiting the world."

Lombardo founded the McGolrick Bird Club in April 2023. Named for Brooklyn, New York's McGolrick Park, the free, weekly meetup convenes every Saturday morning, when he and others go bird watching. The event now has about 100 weekly attendees, and many of them are repeat guests.

Lombardo recently started dabbling with brand partnerships and connected with Offline earlier this year. For him, any partnership would have to benefit the community in some way, he says. But as a freelance graphic designer and writer, it's something he'll consider given the bigger picture of his life and priorities.

"I do need to support my family," he says. "I have two children."

Experts differ on what they think introducing the element of advertising to the groups could mean for the human connection they provide.

Amit Kumar, an assistant professor of marketing, psychological and brain sciences at the University of Delaware, is hopeful about the potential of these events, regardless of a brand's involvement. For him, the point is to create as many opportunities as possible for people to meet.

"As long as they actually are allowing people to engage with one another and connect with one another," he says, "there's a potential benefit to that."

Professor of sociology at Cornell University Benjamin Cornwell is not so sure. A community that got together organically through a genuine, shared interest is no longer organic once fueled and harnessed by a profit-motivated company, he says.

When that happens, community organizers risk introducing unwelcome messaging. Members may "lose faith in the original purpose," he says, adding that some will even "drop out." That won't necessarily happen immediately, he says. But, "it's inevitable."

Back at the book crawl, attendees' opinions differ about Tinder's involvement.

Some think it's cool Tinder is branching out into IRL events. Others don't like the implication that the event is somehow a ruse to meet a significant other, rather than a way to just meet people in general. Some people don't mind as long as the company doesn't feel too pushy in its agenda.

In September, Chervin partnered with an AI company at one of her events and appeared in an ad for Meta's AI tool Muse.

But among her larger community, she says, "a few people did tell me they don't think they can come back."

Roth stands by the financial ecosystem Offline is trying to build.

"If 10 years from now people look at us and say, 'Oh, these are the evil guys that bastardized IRL,' so be it," he says. "But I'll look at that and say, you know, we paid out a billion dollars to organizers, and we've created I don't know how many more events."

#2907. Patriots’ Drake Maye links up with Tom Brady for incredible photo before Packers game

Patriots' Drake Maye links up with Tom Brady for incredible photo before Packers game appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

On Sunday afternoon, the New England Patriots will hit the field at home for a big game against the Green Bay Packers. In town to call the game for FOX is Patriots legend Tom Brady, who won six Super Bowls with the franchise during his iconic run there.

Now, the reins are held by quarterback Drake Maye, and before the game, the two linked up for a photo that will have Patriots fans' hearts beaming.

It didn't take long for Maye to make it to his first Super Bowl in New England, doing so in his second year at the helm last season and losing to the Seattle Seahawks.
